## A03 — Database, persistence, migrations and backups

**Source:** `A03_AUDIT_2026-11-10.md` (APPROVED, approved by Rama on 2026-11-11 with the A03 topic package (Decision Register, D-32)), its sections 1.8, 5.3 to 16.3, 17 and 19, with its evidence file `evidence/A03_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-10.md`, both in `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/TOPIC_WISE_DOCUMENTATION/A03_DATABASE_PERSISTENCE_MIGRATIONS_AND_BACKUPS/`. "AUDIT line N" in a cell is the line of that AUDIT, as committed with this register, where the field is recorded in full.

**Entries:** 248, approved by Rama on 2026-11-11 with the A03 topic package (Decision Register, D-32): the 206 ISSUEs (eleven of them KNOWN DEFECTs, provisional, as Rama's treatment decision of 11-Nov-2026 keeps them: P-01, R-04, U-03, U-07, U-12, U-13, U-14, E-09, J-06, J-12 and Z-02) and the 42 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items (D-04, D-18, H-05, H-18, H-20, N-16, N-17, N-18, N-19, P-04, P-08, P-12, P-13, P-14, Q-24, Q-25, Q-26, R-13, R-14, R-16, U-27, U-28, Y-10, Y-11, Y-12, Y-13, E-12, E-13, E-14, E-15, E-16, G-19, G-28, G-29, G-30, J-05, J-10, J-22, Z-22, Z-23, Z-24 and Z-25). Not entered: the 308 MATCH items; the ten WITHDRAWN records, each counted once elsewhere (N-09, Q-02, Q-15, R-15, U-16, U-19, G-14, G-18, G-46 and J-21; AUDIT section 1.8); the pointer rows to A01, A02 and other A03 items; the inherited A01 and A02 items, whose IDs, classifications and treatments are unchanged (AUDIT section 4); and the parts handed to other topics (AUDIT section 17), which their owners record.

**How this section is built:** as the A02 section: the issue category is the AUDIT record's own; the component is the first legacy file of the record's code path (or "several files" where the record names a set); the severity or impact cell gives the opening of the record's own field, or "not established" where the record has none. ChatGPT's review of 10-Nov-2026 passed the AUDIT (step 7), and A03's issue discussion began the same day (step 8). The 8 findings whose classification depends on Rama's intent (D-04, H-05, N-17, N-18, P-04, Y-10, Y-11 and E-12, all UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) were raised in batch 1 on 10-Nov-2026 and entered in the Open Issues Register as A03-01 to A03-08: Rama answered every history question OPEN, so they stay UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and accepted ChatGPT's new-system suggestions as Phase 2 inputs; he confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026. In batch 2, on 10-Nov-2026, the intent questions on database access, transactions and schema were raised (Open Issues Register A03-09 to A03-27): Rama answered every history question OPEN and accepted ChatGPT's new-system suggestions as Phase 2 inputs; he confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026. The "Raised with Rama" cells of the 27 A03 findings record that they were raised with Rama; their classifications are unchanged. The treatment of the ISSUEs is decided in the treatment round; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items receive no treatment. A02 V-11 (referred to by A03-18) is unchanged. In batch 3, on 11-Nov-2026, the store API and SQL questions were raised (Open Issues Register A03-28 to A03-43): Rama answered every history question and part OPEN and accepted each new-system proposal offered as a Phase 2 input (A03-39's and A03-42's new-system parts were not asked: A03-17's and A03-19's agreed inputs apply, unchanged); he confirmed the read-back on 11-Nov-2026. The "Raised with Rama" cells of the 20 A03 ISSUE and UNKNOWN findings record that they were raised with Rama; their classifications are unchanged (U-12, U-13 and U-14 stay KNOWN DEFECT, provisional; P-08 and U-27 stay UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). H-11 and Q-14, context only, were not raised; MATCH records have no row. In batch 4, on 11-Nov-2026, the checkpoint, retention, backup and restore-drill questions were raised (Open Issues Register A03-44 to A03-51): Rama answered every history question and part OPEN and accepted each new-system suggestion offered as a Phase 2 input (A03-46's new-system part was not asked: A03-07's and A03-08's agreed inputs apply, unchanged), with an additional system-wide input on an end-of-day operational execution report (A03-45); he confirmed the read-back on 11-Nov-2026. The "Raised with Rama" cells of these 18 A03 findings record this; E-06 was not asked (its question part is answered by evidence); their classifications are unchanged (Z-02, J-06 and J-12 stay KNOWN DEFECT, provisional; G-29 stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). Z-21, covered by A03-02's agreed input, was not raised; MATCH records have no row. Every STUDY question has now been raised or is covered. In the treatment round of 11-Nov-2026, Rama decided the treatment of all 206 ISSUEs in 14 groups ("A03 treatment decisions", below), confirmed at the read-back on 11-Nov-2026: each ISSUE's treatment cell names its group and its status is AGREED; the UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items have no treatment.

### A03 treatment decisions

The treatment round of 11-Nov-2026 (Document 04, section 4, step 8): the 206 ISSUEs in 14 groups, each ISSUE in exactly one group. Each group: Claude's recommendation as put to Rama (PROPOSED until his choice), ChatGPT's suggested choice with its qualification ("CHATGPT_A03_TREATMENT_ROUND_REVIEW_2026-11-11"), and Rama's choice. Rama adopted ChatGPT's suggested choices for all 14 groups (his selection in the Claude chat on 11-Nov-2026: "Adopt all as suggested (Recommended)"); ChatGPT's review of the read-back passed it, and Rama confirmed the read-back on 11-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed"). Where a qualification narrows or limits the recommendation, the qualification governs. Agreed inputs A03-01 to A03-51 and the named A01 and A02 treatments are applied, not reopened; history questions stay OPEN; the 11 KNOWN DEFECTs stay provisional; no classification changed. Every treatment is a Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision and not implementation authorisation; its design details are for Phase 2.

- **Group TG1 — Database access, paths and opening (20 findings: D-01, D-02, D-03, D-05, D-06, D-07, D-08, D-09, D-16, H-19, Y-02, Y-03, Y-04, Y-40, J-18, Z-04, Z-05, Z-20, Q-13, N-08).** Recommendation (Claude): "One database access layer for the whole system, with fixed (project-anchored or configured) paths; read-only processes open read-only; no opener ever creates a missing production database (a missing file is a named failure); schema changes are applied only by the migration process, never by an ordinary open; each database is opened explicitly, and an optional one (analytics) cannot block trading; reads never take the write lock; the writers of the live database and their concurrency rule are declared. Applies A03-09, A03-10 and A03-11 (agreed inputs) and A01-05 (N-08)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept the principles: one governed database-access contract; fixed, explicit paths; read-only access for read-only work; missing production databases fail clearly rather than being silently created; ordinary opens do not run schema migrations; analytics is not an unconditional trading dependency; read operations do not take write locks; and writer/concurrency rules are explicit." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: ""one database access layer" is a required consistency and control boundary, not a decision that every operation must be implemented in one class or one physical module. Phase 2 may select the structure. Preserve the already agreed A03-09, A03-10, A03-11 and A01-05 inputs." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG2 — Transactions, atomicity, failure propagation and connections (21 findings: D-10, D-11, D-12, D-13, D-17, P-11, Q-06, Q-07, Q-10, Q-11, Q-12, Q-18, R-05, R-08, U-20, U-22, U-24, N-04, Y-39, Y-05, E-09).** Recommendation (Claude): "Each business operation that must be all-or-nothing is one transaction, or a documented, resumable sequence with a recorded state after each step; storage failures surface as named errors with a defined outcome (A03-13); connections are owned and closed by their thread or process (A03-14); in-memory state changes only after the database write succeeds, or is reconciled on failure; a broker action followed by a failed database write is recorded for reconciliation, never lost; nothing reports success for a write that failed. Applies A01 group A (failure contract)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept atomicity for each business operation that must be all-or-nothing; named and observable storage failures; clear connection ownership and closure; no success indication after a failed write; and reconciliation when a broker-side action succeeds but persistence fails." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "an external broker action cannot be made atomic with a local database transaction. Such workflows need explicit durable intermediate states, idempotency and reconciliation/recovery. Do not impose one giant transaction on long-running or cross-system workflows. Phase 2 determines the appropriate transaction boundaries and resumable state model. Preserve A03-13, A03-14 and the applicable A01 group A input." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG3 — WAL checkpoint maintenance (7 findings: D-14, D-15, Z-16, Z-17, Z-18, Z-19, Z-21).** Recommendation (Claude): "As agreed in A03-44 (non-blocking, bounded maintenance with reported outcome), A03-45 (scheduled and manual runs told apart; no silent skip; the end-of-day operational report) and A03-02 (each database a separate maintenance target): a maintenance run reports done, busy, skipped (with reason) or failed truthfully; any listed auto-fix exists and is tested; descriptions match the schedule." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept truthful outcomes (done, busy, skipped with reason, failed), separate scheduled and manual-run behaviour, no silent skip, accurate schedule descriptions, and reporting of each database maintenance target. Preserve the agreed A03-44, A03-45 and A03-02 inputs. A listed auto-fix must correspond to a real implemented and tested check." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG4 — Schema constraints and data model (10 findings: H-02, H-03, H-04, H-09, H-12, H-13, Q-01, Q-16, Q-17, P-09).** Recommendation (Claude): "The schema states and enforces its rules at the strongest suitable layer: NOT NULL primary keys, declared relations, mutation policies (A03-16), one current authoritative record where readers rely on one (A03-20), critical rules at the strongest reliable layer (A03-03), constraint violations never reported as duplicates; one stated cardinality per relation (for example entry legs per trade); records that need history keep it; redundant indexes removed. Index and key design is Phase 2." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept explicit key/nullability and relationship rules; enforce declared mutation policies; preserve records where history is required; avoid treating constraint failures as duplicates; and define relationship cardinality and uniqueness where consumers depend on them." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "do not select specific keys, indexes, table structures, cardinalities or history models in this treatment round unless already settled by an agreed input. The responsible business meaning and data ownership must be established first; schema design belongs to Phase 2. Preserve A03-03, A03-16 and A03-20 as recorded." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG5 — Status vocabularies and state transitions (26 findings: H-06, H-07, H-08, P-01, P-02, P-03, P-05, P-06, P-07, Q-03, Q-05, Q-08, Q-09, Q-21, R-01, R-02, R-03, R-04, R-06, R-07, R-09, R-10, R-11, U-05, U-06, U-08).** Recommendation (Claude): "One authoritative vocabulary and transition map per status field, enforced in one place (A03-17, A03-30, A03-31); every status write states its allowed from-states and reports a stale target; a missing completion record is detected and handled (A03-22); one stated write rule per field (A03-28); shared definitions for the active, open and terminal sets instead of copied literals (A03-32 to A03-34); confirmed broker facts (fills, final states) are never overwritten by later or weaker writes; each latch has an owner and a clearing path; required status time stamps are stored." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept authoritative status vocabularies and transition rules; guarded from-state transitions; detection of missing or stale target rows; shared definitions for active/open/terminal sets; preservation of confirmed broker facts; and explicit ownership and clearing rules for latches." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: ""one place" means one authoritative definition and enforcement contract, not necessarily one central runtime component. The state machine must be defined per business entity and workflow; do not merge distinct order, trade, EOD or job states merely to make vocabularies uniform. Preserve A03-17, A03-22, A03-28, A03-30 to A03-34 and related agreed inputs." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG6 — Time stamps and dates (11 findings: H-11, Q-14, Q-19, R-12, U-03, U-07, U-26, Y-06, Y-07, G-04, G-23).** Recommendation (Claude): "One IST time source (A01 group D) and one canonical, time-zone-defined stored form for every time stamp and date, with consistent parsing, comparison and ordering rules (A03-43 for candles); no host-clock or database-default time anywhere; date filters use the stored form, never SQL DATE() on offset text. The format itself is chosen in Phase 2." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one authoritative time source consistent with the A01 decision; explicit timezone semantics; consistent parsing, comparison and ordering; and no accidental dependence on host-local time or database defaults. Date filtering must respect the stored timestamp's semantics." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "do not decide the storage representation (UTC, IST-offset ISO-8601, or another explicit canonical representation) in Phase 1. Define timestamp purpose and semantics first; select and validate the representation in Phase 2. Preserve A03-43 and the A01 group D input." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG7 — Unused tables, methods and code (10 findings: H-14, H-15, H-16, H-17, H-49, H-50, Q-22, N-15, G-05, G-17).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every table, method, file and job has a declared purpose, producer and consumer; anything without one is wired to a stated use or retired, with the decision recorded (A03-36, A03-37); planned API is kept only when documented and tested; dashboards never present an empty, unwritten table as live data." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept that every retained table, method, file and job must have a declared purpose, owner, producer/consumer relationship and lifecycle; dashboards must not portray unwritten data as live." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "absence of a current caller or writer is evidence, not by itself authority to delete or retire something. Determine whether the item is obsolete, planned, dormant, externally consumed or genuinely unused. Any retirement must be an explicit Phase 2 decision supported by dependency and impact checks. Preserve A03-36 and A03-37." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG8 — Migrations and schema versioning (12 findings: H-01, N-01, N-02, N-03, N-05, N-06, N-10, N-11, N-12, N-13, N-14, Y-01).** Recommendation (Claude): "One versioned migration process for each database (analytics included, A03-27), with one version authority (A03-15); each step all-or-nothing and verified (A03-23); an absent version marker is handled by telling an empty database from a non-empty one (A03-24), and an unreadable one stops safely (A03-25), never a fresh start; a stated lowest supported version (A03-26); counters and data preserved; a failed migration stops and alerts once, without a retry loop; dry runs and status checks write nothing (A01-05)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept controlled, versioned migrations for each database; one version authority per database; safe handling of missing or unreadable version markers; validated migration steps; preservation of data and counters; truthful failure and stop behaviour; and dry-run/status operations that do not mutate operational state." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "do not imply that changes spanning separate SQLite database files can be one atomic transaction. Define per-database atomic steps and an explicit resumable/reconciliation contract for cross-database changes. Decide supported starting versions and migration mechanics in Phase 2. Preserve A03-15, A03-23 to A03-27 and A01-05." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG9 — Failure visibility: logging, heartbeats, exit codes and dry runs (24 findings: Q-04, Q-20, G-24, G-25, Z-02, J-06, Z-06, Z-07, Z-11, Z-12, Z-13, Z-14, U-02, U-04, U-17, U-18, U-21, U-23, G-02, G-15, G-58, G-59, E-48, J-16).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every failure leaves a bounded, dated, attributable trace (A03-38); logging is configured in every process under one written logging contract (A02 V-13); exit codes and heartbeats state the real outcome, failure included, and a failure is distinct from success and from a missed run (A03-50); dry runs write nothing and preview exactly what the action would do (A03-48); monitoring reads each job's declared completion signal only; the end-of-day operational execution report (A03-45) shows every suitable job's real result." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept attributable failure records; configured logging in every process; truthful exit codes and completion signals; distinction between success, partial completion, failure and missed execution; dry-run previews that accurately represent the action; and the agreed end-of-day operational execution report." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "a dry run must not change operational state or trigger the action being simulated. Harmless preview output (for example stdout or an explicitly requested report) is not the same as mutating state; its permitted outputs must be defined. "Every failure is alerted once" should be implemented through bounded, deduplicated reporting, not by making alert delivery a prerequisite for recording the failure. Preserve A03-38, A03-45, A03-48, A03-50 and A02 V-13." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG10 — File persistence: atomic writes, locks and sentinels (25 findings: E-01, E-02, E-03, E-04, E-07, E-08, E-11, E-47, G-01, G-03, G-06, G-07, G-08, G-09, G-10, G-11, G-12, G-13, G-16, G-20, G-21, G-22, G-26, G-27, G-57).** Recommendation (Claude): "One file-persistence rule: write to a unique temporary file, flush and rename; append-only logs write whole records; every lock records its owner and is released only by its owner, with stale-lock rules; each folder and file path comes from one configured source; alert sentinels are written whatever the delivery channel settings, and a write failure is itself alerted once; an unreadable state file is a named failure, not a silent fallback; concurrent writers of one state file are designed out." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept safe atomic replacement for state files; complete append records; ownership-aware locks and release; one authoritative configured path per state family; no silent fallback from unreadable state; and sentinel recording independent of the notification channel's enablement." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "choose the concrete atomic-write, durability and locking mechanisms according to the filesystem, process model and deployment environment in Phase 2. Do not treat every file as the same persistence class: distinguish replaceable state, append-only evidence, logs, locks, markers and credentials. This recommendation does not authorise a separate credential/security investigation or disclosure. Preserve A03-48 and the relevant A01/A02 inputs." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG11 — Retention (13 findings: Z-01, Z-03, Z-08, Z-09, Z-10, Z-15, E-05, E-06, U-09, U-10, J-07, J-08, J-09).** Recommendation (Claude): "As agreed in A03-07, A03-08 and A03-46 (an explicit retention or preservation rule for every family), A03-47 (capital-ledger records kept while anything depends on them) and A03-48 (dated records of every run; a stable selection key; enforced protection of never-delete families): every retention input is validated before any deletion; the newest copy of a family is never deleted; a database file and its sidecars are handled as one unit; logs rotate by date so age-based rules work." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept validated retention inputs before deletion; explicit preservation rules for each data family; protection of capital-ledger records while dependencies remain; and treating a database plus its required sidecars as one retention unit." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "do not impose one universal age rule or require every family to rotate by trading date. Each family needs its own purpose-based retention/preservation rule, stable selection key, dependency check and safe deletion rule. "Never delete the newest copy" is a safety guard, not a substitute for a complete retention policy. Preserve A03-07, A03-08, A03-46, A03-47 and A03-48." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG12 — Backups and restore (14 findings: J-01, J-02, J-03, J-04, J-11, J-12, J-13, J-14, J-15, J-17, J-19, J-20, J-45, Y-09).** Recommendation (Claude): "As agreed in A03-49 (truthful drill outcomes, coverage, age and schema-based checks), A03-50 (one completion signal per job) and A03-51 (a verified independent off-machine copy covering everything needed to restore): a written, tested restore procedure (program or runbook) is part of the system; backup jobs never leave partial copies unreported; documented cadences and retention match the code." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept a documented and tested restore procedure; verified independent off-machine protection for everything needed to restore; truthful backup/drill outcomes; coverage checks based on the actual schema and dependencies; and consistency between declared and implemented backup cadence and retention." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "the backup scope, destinations, cadence, retention, verification method, recovery-point objective and recovery-time objective must be established as Phase 2 requirements. Do not assume a specific provider, storage technology or cadence now. A backup is not proven usable merely because a file exists; restore verification must test the actual recovery path. Preserve A03-49, A03-50 and A03-51." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG13 — Descriptions and documents against code (6 findings: N-07, P-10, Q-23, H-10, Y-08, E-10).** Recommendation (Claude): "Descriptions match the code and are checked for consistency (A01 group J); a promised behaviour is either implemented or removed from the description." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept that comments, docstrings, banners, labels and operational documentation must accurately describe established behaviour." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "where documentation states an intended behaviour that code does not implement, do not automatically change the documentation to match the defect. First distinguish intended requirement from stale description. The owner decides whether the behaviour is required, should be removed, or remains open; the resulting documentation and implementation inputs must agree. Preserve the A01 group J input." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **Group TG14 — Queries and scripts against the schema (7 findings: U-01, U-11, U-12, U-13, U-14, U-15, U-25).** Recommendation (Claude): "As agreed in A03-12 (no unguarded bulk changes; any operator recovery is a distinct, authorised, confirmable and audited workflow), A03-19 and A03-42 (one journal row per date and trade; atomic rebuild) and A03-41 (a schema or query mismatch is a failure, never an empty result): every query is checked against the schema it runs on (tested in CI); recovery claims ("a later run picks it up") are true or removed." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Accepted principle, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept schema-aware validation of queries, including automated tests against the schema versions they are expected to use; schema/query mismatches must surface as failures rather than being silently represented as empty results. Preserve the agreed operator-recovery boundary: no unguarded bulk changes; recovery is a distinct, authorised, confirmable and audited workflow. Preserve A03-12, A03-19, A03-41 and A03-42." Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "define the test coverage and supported schema-version matrix in Phase 2. Do not assume that a static text search alone proves runtime compatibility." Status: AGREED (11-Nov-2026).
- **The 42 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items:** no treatment. MATCH records have no row and no treatment.

## A04 — Broker interface, authentication and broker state

**Source:** `A04_AUDIT_2026-11-13.md` (DRAFT; passed by ChatGPT's review of 13-Nov-2026, Document 04, section 4, step 7, at `a60b3d0`), its sections 1.8, 5.3 to 14.3, 15 and 17, with its evidence file `evidence/A04_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-12.md`, both in `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/TOPIC_WISE_DOCUMENTATION/A04_BROKER_INTERFACE_AUTHENTICATION_AND_BROKER_STATE/`. "AUDIT line N" in a cell is the line of that AUDIT, as committed with this register, where the field is recorded in full.

**Entries:** 198, proposed and not approved: the 178 ISSUEs (four of them KNOWN DEFECTs, provisional, as Rama's treatment decision of 13-Nov-2026 keeps them: RD-01, RL-03, MO-03 and MO-09) and the 20 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items (CL-13, CL-18, AU-04, AU-12, TT-14, TT-28, OA-15, OA-27, RD-08, RD-16, RL-11, RL-12, RL-13, RL-14, MO-11, MO-14, MO-21, PR-14, CG-14 and CG-15). MO-04 is the A04 side of A03 R-04: it is entered as an A04 ISSUE, and R-04's classification (KNOWN DEFECT, provisional), status and treatment stay A03's, unchanged. Not entered: the 144 MATCH items; the four WITHDRAWN records, each counted once elsewhere (CS-06 as OA-22, CS-09 as RD-12, MO-18 as RD-07 and TT-29 as RL-06; AUDIT section 1.8); the pointer rows to A01, A02, A03 and other A04 items; the inherited A01, A02 and A03 items and the Open Issues Register entries that name A04, whose IDs, classifications, statuses and treatments are unchanged (AUDIT section 4); and the parts handed to other topics (AUDIT section 15), which their owners record.

**How this section is built:** as the A03 section: the issue category is the AUDIT record's own; the component is the first legacy file of the record's code path (or "several files" where the record names a set); the severity or impact cell gives the opening of the record's own field, or "not established" where the record has none. ChatGPT's review passed the AUDIT on 13-Nov-2026 (step 7, at `a60b3d0`), and A04's issue discussion began the same day (step 8). Batch 1 (Open Issues Register A04-01 to A04-04) was raised with Rama on 13-Nov-2026 and its read-back confirmed by him the same day: the "Raised with Rama" cells of AU-04, AU-07, AU-12 and AU-13 record it; their classifications are unchanged. Batch 2 (Open Issues Register A04-05 to A04-15) was raised on 13-Nov-2026 and its read-back confirmed by him the same day: the "Raised with Rama" cells of CL-11, CL-14, AU-06, AU-07 (beside its batch 1 entry), AU-10, CS-03, MO-07, TT-11, TT-12, TT-20, TT-21, TT-22, TT-24 and TT-28 record it; AU-24 and TT-40 (A04-08 and A04-15) are MATCH items, with no row here; no classification changed. Batch 3 (Open Issues Register A04-16 to A04-26) was raised on 13-Nov-2026 and its read-back confirmed by him the same day: the "Raised with Rama" cells of OA-08, OA-15 (related, under A04-17), OA-16, OA-20, RD-05, RD-06, RD-07, MO-01, MO-13, MO-16, MO-17 and OA-22 record it; MO-40 (A04-24) is a MATCH item, with no row here; no classification changed. Batch 4 (Open Issues Register A04-27 to A04-37) was raised on 13-Nov-2026 and its corrected read-back confirmed by him the same day: the "Raised with Rama" cells of RL-01, RL-02, RL-03, RL-06, RL-14, PR-05, PR-10, PR-11, CG-05, CG-08 and CG-14 record it; no classification changed. With it the intent round is complete (the 37 planned entries, A04-01 to A04-37); the other findings are taken up at the treatment round. The treatment round of 13-Nov-2026 decided the treatment of every ISSUE ("A04 treatment decisions", below): each ISSUE's treatment cell names its group, and its status is AGREED; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items receive no treatment.

### A04 treatment decisions

The treatment round of 13-Nov-2026 (Document 04, section 4, step 8; the step 8 plan, Part 2): the 178 ISSUEs in 28 groups (TG1 to TG28, ordered by the plan's batches T1 to T6; TG28, the hard-coded values, spans five batches), each ISSUE in exactly one group. Each group: Claude's recommendation as put to Rama (PROPOSED until his choice; seven recommendations adjusted for ChatGPT's cross-cutting clarifications before they were put to him), ChatGPT's suggested choice with its qualification ("CHATGPT_A04_TREATMENT_ROUND_PLAN_REVIEW_2026-11-13"; TG25's sentence on Rama's rule as corrected at ChatGPT's final review of the file), and Rama's choice. Rama adopted ChatGPT's suggested choices for all 28 groups (his selection in the Claude chat on 13-Nov-2026: "Adopt all as suggested (Recommended)", described to him as each group taking its own suggested letter and qualification); ChatGPT's review of the read-back passed it, and Rama confirmed the read-back on 13-Nov-2026 ("Confirmed as written"). Where a qualification narrows or limits the recommendation, the qualification governs. Agreed inputs A04-01 to A04-37 (with Rama's additional inputs at A04-28 and A04-33) and the named A01, A02 and A03 treatments are applied, not reopened; history questions stay OPEN; the four KNOWN DEFECTs (RD-01, RL-03, MO-03, MO-09) stay provisional; MO-04 follows A03 R-04's agreed treatment (A03 TG5); no classification changed; cross-topic decisions stay with their owners (products and brokers: Rama and A10; the sizing source: A09; recovery: A12; kill semantics: A13). Every treatment is a Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision.

- **Group TG1 (T1) — The broker boundary and the adapter contract (5 findings: CL-01, CL-03, CL-10, CL-16, CL-17).** Recommendation (Claude): "One broker boundary: every broker call goes through one adapter interface with a written public contract (methods, results, errors); no module reaches the SDK or the adapter's private members around it; an unbuilt or stub broker (the AngelOne stub) is either completed behind the same interface or explicitly marked unsupported, so no text calls it built; a broker-neutral interface is defined only if more than one broker is in scope (Rama's scope decision)." Applies: A04-12 (one adapter boundary); A04-08 and A01-03, A01-24 (one configured account); A02-15's history stays OPEN. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one governed broker-call boundary and a documented public contract; no component should bypass it through direct SDK calls or private members. Keep the broker-specific implementation behind that boundary. Do not decide a broker-neutral/multi-broker abstraction now: broker scope remains Rama's decision and A10's scope. The AngelOne stub must not be represented as a completed capability; its status/scope is for the relevant scope decision."

- **Group TG2 (T1) — Client construction, credentials and the account (10 findings: CL-02, CL-04, CL-05, CL-07, CL-08, CL-09, CL-11, CL-12, CL-14, CL-15).** Recommendation (Claude): "One construction path for every broker client, the scripts included: it validates the mode, the client and the presence of the credentials (by name, never by value) before any call, and refuses an empty key or token; the API key comes from the one configured account (one account at a time: no multiple account or credential records, no switching; changing the account means the user replaces the one account's data; no second authoritative copy of the key in the token file); the client takes a new token without a restart where the lifecycle allows it; every configured timeout is honoured; one stated SDK version rule; a malformed token file is a named failure; the T2 script's own client follows the same rules (A19 owns the script)." Applies: A04-05 (one authoritative account configuration), A04-08 (one account at a time), A04-01 (token lifecycle); A01 group H (configuration, credentials and accounts). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one consistent client-construction and credential-validation path, including scripts where in scope. Enforce the already-agreed one-account-at-a-time rule: one configured account only, no multiple account records or account switching; changing account means replacing the existing account data. The API key must have one authoritative configured source; token-file metadata must not silently become a second source of truth. Never expose credential values in logs. Do not select an SDK version or concrete timeout values in this round."

- **Group TG3 (T1) — The token file, its readers and the login (4 findings: AU-01, AU-02, AU-03, AU-05).** Recommendation (Claude): "One token-file reader and one validation contract used by every consumer (gate, watcher, tools, scripts): a defined file format, typed failures for an unreadable or malformed file, the account identity and the applicable expiry checked; the login re-prompts on an empty request token and states its failures; the login tool runs standalone without path tricks." Applies: A04-13 (one shared token-validation contract), A04-01 (token lifecycle contract), A04-05. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one shared token-file format, reader and validation contract for all consumers, including account association and an explicit expiry basis. Malformed, unreadable, missing, stale or mismatched-account data must produce distinguishable failures, not silent defaults. Login must reject an empty request token and report its failure clearly. Do not infer that local file deletion equals broker token expiry; retain the already-agreed separation between broker validity and local retention."

- **Group TG4 (T1) — In-session authentication: the token monitor and authentication failures (7 findings: AU-06, AU-08, AU-09, AU-10, AU-11, AU-13, AU-14).** Recommendation (Claude): "One co-ordinated authentication-failure policy across the client, token monitor, order monitor, reconciler, clock probe and start path, with failures classed from structured broker information, not message text; the monitor's check goes through the adapter boundary; a soft kill that fails is not latched as done (its retry or escalation follows A13's kill semantics); whether and how the running process takes a new token is defined in the token lifecycle; alerts describe what actually happens to order monitoring; texts match the code." Applies: A04-09 (co-ordinated policy, with A13), A04-04 (structured classification), A04-12, A04-02 and A04-03 (first check; lifecycle and detectors); A01 C3 and A01-08 not reopened. Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept coordinated authentication-failure classification using structured broker information rather than message-text matching, and route broker calls through the governed adapter boundary. Define the consequences for each failure class, including whether trading stops, what order supervision continues, and what alerts state. However, retry/escalation and kill behaviour must remain consistent with A13's kill semantics and the agreed recovery scope. Do not decide token replacement in a running process independently of the token lifecycle. Do not weaken or override A01 C3 or A01-08."

- **Group TG5 (T1) — The broker clock reading (6 findings: CL-06, AU-15, AU-16, AU-17, AU-18, AU-19).** Recommendation (Claude): "The Date-header capture is installed with a record and reports its own failures; a broker-time reading is marked as broker time or as a local fallback, and the start-up check never passes on a fallback treated as broker time; each reading belongs to its own call; failures keep their own error identity; any round-trip estimate starts after the limiter's wait; texts describe the current method." Applies: A01 group D (time, calendar and clock); A04-09 (the probe within the authentication policy). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept explicit provenance for broker time versus local fallback, and ensure a fallback cannot be represented as a successful broker-time check. Preserve per-call reading identity and distinct error identity. Any round-trip estimate must account for limiter waiting. Exact thresholds, acceptance windows and startup consequences remain for Phase 2 and must align with A01's time/calendar rules."

- **Group TG6 (T2) — Token production: the headless refresh (9 findings: TT-01, TT-02, TT-03, TT-04, TT-05, TT-06, TT-08, TT-09, TT-27).** Recommendation (Claude): "One controlled token-production path with a single declared schedule that its texts repeat; manual recovery an explicitly controlled exception, not a second routine path; HTTP failures classed one way at every step; the second-factor retry reaches a fresh code window; a total time limit that fits any caller's limit (the pre-flight); logging configured, so success and failure both leave a record; one path-anchoring rule; the credential sources described as coded; the local token-file deletion schedule kept separate from broker expiry and stated for the days it applies." Applies: A04-11 (one token-production path), A04-01 (local retention distinct from broker expiry), A04-05; A02 V-13 (one logging contract); A03 TG1 and TG10 (fixed paths; file persistence). Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one controlled and observable token-production path, consistent outcome classification, bounded execution, fresh second-factor retry handling, and auditable success/failure records. Apply the agreed separation of local token-file cleanup from broker expiry. Do not freeze a particular schedule or manual-run rule here. Manual recovery must be an explicitly controlled exception, not a parallel routine token-production path; reconcile with the agreed history that BAT-copy existed first and headless creation was added later. Never put secrets in logs."

- **Group TG7 (T2) — Token freshness, account selection and copying (4 findings: TT-10, TT-11, TT-12, TT-24).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every tool selects the one configured account the same way and judges a token by the shared validation contract (account, presence and shape, expiry basis), so no starter starts a service its own gate will refuse; token files are published through one atomic write path; the incident procedure for a mid-session token failure cannot silently reuse the failed token." Applies: A04-13, A04-08, A04-14 (one atomic publication path), A04-11 (controlled fallback only after a failed or missing refresh); A03 E-11 kept. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one account-selection rule and the shared token-validation contract, with one atomic token publication/update path. All consumers must apply the same freshness and account checks. A failed token must not be silently reused. Any fallback procedure must be controlled, observable and consistent with the accepted single-account rule and the token-production policy. Preserve A03 E-11 as already agreed."

- **Group TG8 (T2) — The token watcher, the service start and the installer (9 findings: TT-07, TT-13, TT-15, TT-16, TT-17, TT-18, TT-19, TT-25, TT-26).** Recommendation (Claude): "One authoritative service-start policy (IST boundaries; any narrower watcher window a documented guardrail); a refused early start does not hold the day; alerts state the real cause; all dates in one time zone; the watcher's state survives the loss of a temporary folder or is re-created with a record; no silent hold (a held start leaves a trace); operator instructions match the sanctioned resume procedure; the installer and operator texts state what actually starts the service; a failed or missing refresh is observable." Applies: A04-15 (one start policy), A04-11; A01 groups D and J; A03 E-47 kept. Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one authoritative service-start policy and consistent time-zone handling, with observable refused/held starts, failed refreshes and watcher-state recovery. A narrower watcher window may be a guardrail only if its purpose and relationship to the main service window are explicit. Do not decide the final service operating hours or alter A01's approved orchestration rules here. Resume instructions must follow the separately approved recovery procedure; preserve A03 E-47."

- **Group TG9 (T2) — The PC morning scripts and their runbooks (5 findings: TT-20, TT-21, TT-22, TT-23, TT-49).** Recommendation (Claude): "If PC tools are kept at all, they are the controlled fallback only (after a failed or missing refresh), check the service state exactly, wait for the watcher's actual cycle before judging a start, are described accurately (no false "equivalent"), and runbooks name the real files and alert titles." Applies: A04-11, A04-13, A04-14; A01 group J. Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept PC scripts, if retained, only as a controlled fallback rather than a parallel routine token path. They must check the actual service and refresh state, wait for the relevant watcher cycle, and report accurate outcomes. This is a Phase 2 principle, not a historical conclusion: Rama's recollection that BAT-copy existed before headless refresh does not establish when PC scripts should run. Do not treat the legacy chronology as a new-system requirement."

- **Group TG10 (T3) — Exception translation and failure identity (9 findings: OA-01, OA-02, OA-03, OA-04, OA-17, OA-19, RD-03, RD-04, RD-11).** Recommendation (Claude): "Broker failures are translated into structured outcome classes by operation (refusal, rejection by input, transport timeout, authentication, rate limit, unknown outcome, invalid data), with broker error codes kept apart from HTTP status; local conversion or parsing errors are never reported as broker errors and never escape untranslated; a missing broker answer (order id, trigger id, list) is a failure, not a default; every error carries the agreed context keys." Applies: A04-18 (refusal against unknown outcome; structured result), A04-20 (response validation; typed errors), A04-04; A01 group I (internal events and error types). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept structured, operation-aware outcomes that distinguish broker refusal/rejection, transport timeout, authentication, rate limit, unknown outcome, invalid broker data and local parsing/conversion errors. Missing required response fields must not be converted into empty lists, zeros or placeholders. Keep broker error codes separate from HTTP status. The exact taxonomy and context fields are Phase 2 design details."

- **Group TG11 (T3) — 429 handling in the adapter (4 findings: OA-05, OA-06, OA-07, RD-01).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every broker call, cancel and modify included, receives the same 429 handling in the bucket it actually used; each call reports its own operation name; one stated owner repeats an action after a 429, within the governed rate-limit policy. RD-01 stays a KNOWN DEFECT, provisional, as the treatment keeps it." Applies: A04-27 (one governed rate-limit policy), A04-28 (Rama's priority input: priority within each shared limit, no fixed quotas). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept consistent 429 handling for every broker operation, including cancel and modify, with the actual request bucket and operation identified. There must be one governed retry/continuation owner; avoid duplicate retries by both adapter and caller. Apply A04-27 and Rama's priority-aware, shared-limit input at A04-28: prioritise within shared broker limits without fixed quotas. RD-01 remains a provisional KNOWN DEFECT."

- **Group TG12 (T3) — Order action outcomes: placement, cancel and modify (5 findings: OA-08, OA-09, OA-18, OA-20, OA-21).** Recommendation (Claude): "A placement that times out is an unknown submission outcome reconciled against the broker before any retry; a cancel or modify reports a confirmed refusal separately from an unknown outcome; the state record never stays in a stale state after a local failure; analogous actions use one failure-reporting style; the adapter's contract texts match the code." Applies: A04-16 (unknown submission outcome), A04-18; A03 TG5 (status vocabularies and transitions). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept the principle that a timeout or missing acknowledgement creates an unknown outcome, not proof of failure or success. Reconcile with broker state before retrying a potentially duplicated action. Keep confirmed refusal distinct from unknown outcome, and ensure local state cannot remain falsely final after a local processing failure. The exact reconciliation sequence and durable state transitions belong to Phase 2 and must align with A03's agreed status vocabulary."

- **Group TG13 (T3) — Validation, price snapping and tags (8 findings: OA-10, OA-11, OA-12, OA-13, OA-14, OA-24, OA-25, RD-14).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every order action and every read validates its inputs at the boundary, after any snapping (so a validated price is the price sent); one rounding rule per order type, the same for placement and modification; GTT actions validated and snapped like other placements; tags passed through within the broker's documented limits and used only to help correlation, never as an idempotency guarantee; texts describe the code." Applies: A04-16 (tag not an idempotency guarantee); TG28 (one tick-rounding function). Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept validation at the action boundary and consistent price snapping/rounding for placement and modification; validate the final value that will be sent. GTT actions should follow the applicable order validation rules. Tags may support correlation but must not be treated as an idempotency guarantee. Do not choose tick-rounding rules or broker-specific tag limits in this round; carry the shared rounding principle into Phase 2."

- **Group TG14 (T3) — Varieties, market protection and the GTT gate (3 findings: OA-16, OA-22, OA-23).** Recommendation (Claude): "One explicit market-protection policy for every MARKET and SL-M path that needs it, validated before submission; the variety sent always matches the order (a cover-order path exists only if cover orders are in scope); the delivery gate on GTT actions is stated once and the texts match it." Applies: A04-17 (market protection), A04-26 (cover orders only if in approved Live scope; A10 and Rama); OA-15 stays UNKNOWN (the RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD). Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept an explicit, consistently applied market-protection policy wherever the selected order path requires it, and ensure the submitted variety matches the actual order type. Do not decide cover-order support or product/broker scope here; that remains with Rama and A10. Preserve OA-15 as UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. The GTT delivery gate must be reconciled with the applicable approved scope rather than assumed from legacy behaviour."

- **Group TG15 (T3) — Broker reads: shapes, statuses, quotes and margins (8 findings: RD-05, RD-06, RD-07, RD-09, RD-10, RD-12, RD-13, RD-35).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every read validates the response envelope and fields and returns a typed invalid-data outcome instead of an empty list, a zero or a placeholder; one convention for an absent field; non-final broker statuses kept in a normalized lifecycle with the raw status; an absent price is absent, never 0.0; one quote wrapper with one keying convention; a margin read that cannot be computed is a failure, not a cached zero (the sizing source is A09's decision); texts match the code." Applies: A04-20, A04-19 (normalized lifecycle; unknown statuses kept unresolved), A04-21. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept strict response-envelope and field validation. Distinguish an absent/invalid response from a confirmed empty result; do not substitute zero or placeholder values for missing prices or uncomputable margin. Preserve raw broker status alongside normalized status, and keep unknown statuses unresolved rather than forcing a false terminal state. The exact schema and margin source are Phase 2 matters; sizing-source authority remains with A09."

- **Group TG16 (T3) — Adapter logging and operation names (3 findings: OA-26, RD-02, RD-15).** Recommendation (Claude): "The adapter's logging contract applies to every call (entry, exit, failure with traceback, cache hits), and each failure is logged under its own operation name." Applies: A02 V-13 (one logging contract); A03 TG9 (failure visibility). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept a consistent logging contract for broker operations, including operation identity, outcome and relevant failure context. Do not log credentials, access tokens, request secrets or other sensitive values. Cache-hit logging should be proportionate and must not flood operational logs. Keep the detailed event schema and retention policy for the appropriate Phase 2/logging topic."

- **Group TG17 (T4) — Callers’ handling of adapter results and exceptions (6 findings: CS-01, CS-02, CS-03, CS-04, CS-05, CS-12).** Recommendation (Claude): "Callers read every result before changing state; one handling rule per outcome class across callers, inside the co-ordinated authentication-failure and rate-limit policies; no state decision from text substrings; callers catch the documented exceptions; the adapter hands callers its own normalised types, not the SDK's raw payloads." Applies: A04-09, A04-18, A04-20, A04-27. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept a consistent caller contract: inspect the result, handle typed outcomes, and only change system state after the outcome is understood. Avoid text-substring classification and leaking raw SDK payloads to callers. Ensure caller behaviour is coordinated with authentication and rate-limit policies. Do not let this group independently decide kill, recovery or order-supervision semantics owned by A13/A12."

- **Group TG18 (T4) — Wiring and duplicated routes (4 findings: CS-07, CS-08, CS-10, CS-11).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every built capability is wired to an approved use or explicitly identified as unsupported or not in scope, with the decision recorded (whether the live margin read and its invalidation, the in-flight recovery and the marketable-exit pricing are kept is for A09, A12 and Rama); one rule for the marketable-exit price; one route from each component to the adapter." Applies: A03 TG7 (declared purpose or retired); STUDY question 14 answered by evidence (CS-07's treatment). Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept the principle that each capability must have one clear, documented route and be either genuinely wired to an approved use or explicitly identified as unsupported/not in scope. Do not decide here whether the live margin read, in-flight recovery or marketable-exit pricing is retained, retired or redesigned: those depend on A09, A12 and Rama's scope decisions. No capability should be labelled implemented merely because a stub or unused helper exists."

- **Group TG19 (T4) — The rate limiter and the broker limits (10 findings: RL-01, RL-02, RL-03, RL-04, RL-05, RL-06, RL-07, RL-08, RL-09, RL-10).** Recommendation (Claude): "One governed rate-limit policy for the active account and API key, covering every process; one token per broker request; a 429 path for every bucket; a client-side pacing timeout and a shutdown abort are distinct events, not broker failures; correct freeze arithmetic; validated bucket values; stated figures match the code; priority-aware use of each shared limit without fixed quotas. RL-03 stays a KNOWN DEFECT, provisional, as the treatment keeps it." Applies: A04-27, A04-28 (with Rama's priority input), A04-29, A04-30, A04-31; A01-11 and A01 C17 not reopened. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one coordinated policy for all processes using the active account/API key, covering every relevant request bucket and distinguishing broker 429 responses from client-side pacing waits and shutdown aborts. Validate configured limits and use correct freeze arithmetic. Apply Rama's A04-28 input: priority-aware use of shared limits, without fixed quotas. Do not insert numerical rates or quotas in the treatment round. RL-03 remains a provisional KNOWN DEFECT."

- **Group TG20 (T5) — Order status mapping, fills and transitions (11 findings: MO-01, MO-02, MO-03, MO-04, MO-05, MO-06, MO-08, MO-09, MO-10, MO-24, MO-25).** Recommendation (Claude): "One authoritative status vocabulary and transition map, shared by the monitor, the state machine and the consumers, with every non-terminal state given a monitoring policy and bounded escalation; fills taken from the broker's figures and never silently replaced; each committed transition published once; reloaded orders brought forward to their polled state; EXPIRED mapped if it can occur; the broker's rejection text read from the field the broker uses. MO-04 follows A03 R-04's agreed treatment (TG5), not reopened. MO-03 and MO-09 stay KNOWN DEFECTs, provisional, as the treatment keeps them." Applies: A04-19, A04-21 (state-specific deadlines); A03 TG5 and A03-17, A03-30 to A03-34. Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one authoritative status vocabulary and transition policy shared by the monitor, state machine and consumers. Preserve raw broker status, treat unknown states explicitly, source fills from broker-reported figures, and define monitoring/escalation for non-terminal states. Do not decide detailed deadlines or transition mechanics here; reconcile with A03's agreed status model and relevant A13/A12 responsibilities. MO-04 remains governed by A03 R-04's already-agreed treatment and must not be reopened. MO-03 and MO-09 remain provisional KNOWN DEFECTs."

- **Group TG21 (T5) — Cancels, timeouts, the force close and shutdown (7 findings: MO-12, MO-13, MO-15, MO-16, MO-17, MO-20, MO-49).** Recommendation (Claude): "A cancel timeout is an unknown outcome reconciled against the broker, the order kept under monitoring; the broker state is reconciled after a cancel whose outcome or final figures are not confirmed (the conditions set in Phase 2); no order cancelled twice in a cycle; one per-leg timeout policy; the once-per-trading-day force close durable across restarts and reconciled on restart; shutdown stops the poll thread before acting on its orders, and the join is checked." Applies: A04-25, A04-18, A04-22 (per-leg policy), A04-23 (durable force-close state); A01 group E (shutdown). Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept explicit handling of cancel timeouts as unknown outcomes requiring broker reconciliation, and require shutdown coordination so order supervision is not silently abandoned. A once-per-trading-day force-close rule must be durable and reconciled after restart only if that behaviour is within Rama-approved scope. Per-leg timeout values, force-close details and shutdown/kill semantics must be resolved with A12/A13 and A01; do not decide them here. Avoid an unconditional statement that the broker must always be re-read after every cancel unless the Phase 2 design establishes the applicable conditions."

- **Group TG22 (T5) — Failure counters, callbacks and events (4 findings: MO-07, MO-19, MO-26, MO-27).** Recommendation (Claude): "Failure counters count what their rule states (cycles, not polls) and fire once; the orphan callback contract carries the cause; a failing subscriber does not leave a completed order watched; unused fields are removed or wired." Applies: A04-09; A01 group I (internal events). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept counters whose definitions match what they count, single and observable threshold actions, explicit callback cause/context, and safe handling of failing subscribers. A completed order must not remain watched solely because a callback failed. Unused fields should be removed or connected only through an approved design. Exact thresholds and event schema remain open for Phase 2."

- **Group TG23 (T5) — The monitor’s and the state machine’s texts (2 findings: MO-22, MO-23).** Recommendation (Claude): "The monitor's and the state machine's descriptions match the code, or the promised behaviour is implemented." Applies: A01 group J; A03 TG13. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept that operational descriptions, alerts and documentation must accurately describe implemented behaviour. If a behaviour is intended but not implemented, it must not be described as already working. This is a documentation/observability principle, not permission to implement the legacy behaviour."

- **Group TG24 (T6) — Product resolution and the product map (10 findings: PR-01, PR-02, PR-03, PR-04, PR-05, PR-06, PR-07, PR-08, PR-09, PR-24).** Recommendation (Claude): "One authoritative product taxonomy and one governed mapping used in both directions; the map validated at construction; one resolution per order, visible to every gate (including any intraday-only coercion); variety and product checked together; one exception type per failure; texts match the code. Which products are in scope (NRML, CO) is Rama's and A10's decision." Applies: A04-37 (one authoritative mapping); A04-26. Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one authoritative, validated product taxonomy/mapping and consistent resolution used by all relevant order paths. Product and broker support, including NRML/CO and any coercion, is not decided by this group; it remains Rama's decision and A10's scope. Do not assume that legacy product codes or unused branches belong in the new system. Preserve the distinction between a failed resolution and a valid product selection."

- **Group TG25 (T6) — Position helpers and netting (4 findings: PR-10, PR-11, PR-12, PR-13).** Recommendation (Claude): "Positions are identified and reconciled by exact instrument and product; one rule derives a symbol's broker position; a failed positions read is recorded and distinguishable from a confirmed empty one, without weakening the documented safe exit; texts match the code; Rama's rule applies: "A stock whose trade has been closed at the broker today must not be traded again today, whether MIS or CNC. The restriction is stock-wide, not product-specific. Different stocks may still be traded on the same day."" Applies: A04-33 (with Rama's additional input on traded stocks), A04-32. Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept exact instrument/product identification and consistent position derivation/reconciliation. A failed positions read must remain distinguishable from a confirmed empty position. Apply Rama's explicit rule: a stock whose trade has been closed at the broker today must not be traded again that day, whether MIS or CNC. The restriction is stock-wide, not product-specific; different stocks may still be traded on the same day. Do not weaken a safe-exit rule because a read failed; its precise safe behaviour must be resolved with the relevant risk/order topics. Do not infer broader futures/options netting policy beyond the stated input."

- **Group TG26 (T6) — The cost model (10 findings: CG-01, CG-02, CG-03, CG-04, CG-05, CG-06, CG-07, CG-08, CG-09, CG-13).** Recommendation (Claude): "One cost computation from the executed side, product, segment, exchange and an effective-dated tariff; the entry execution charged once and allocated across closed quantity; inputs validated; one product source; a costing failure visible and reconciled, not a silent zero; unused branches and F&O costing retired or scoped; load-time rules match the formula; results reconciled with contract notes." Applies: A04-34, A04-36, A04-35 (contract-note evidence point carried to the RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD). Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept a transparent cost computation using the actual execution context and an effective-dated tariff, with entry costs charged once and allocated consistently to closed quantity. Invalid inputs or unavailable tariff data must not silently produce zero cost. Reconcile computed costs against contract-note evidence. Do not choose fee values, tariff schedules, product coverage or F&O scope here; product scope remains with Rama/A10 and details remain for Phase 2. Preserve CG-14 as UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED and carry the contract-note evidence point to the RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD as directed."

- **Group TG27 (T6) — Slippage and tick rounding (3 findings: CG-10, CG-11, CG-12).** Recommendation (Claude): "One tick-rounding function, with its fallback rule in one place; the slippage model's role decided (wired to a stated consumer or retired); its texts match the code." Applies: TG13; A03 TG7. Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept one authoritative tick-rounding function and a single, explicit owner for the slippage model's purpose. Do not decide the rounding algorithm, fallback values, slippage formula or whether a particular consumer should use the model here. That depends on Phase 2 evidence and the relevant strategy/order topics. A component must not be called active merely because it exists."

- **Group TG28 (T1-T6) — Hard-coded values (8 findings: CL-25, RD-28, RL-16, TT-35, TT-46, CG-30, MO-46, AU-07).** Recommendation (Claude): "Operationally variable settings become validated configuration (config.yaml), not source literals (true invariants and protocol-defined constants may stay code constants): the connection pool, the margin cache lifetime, the limiter's wait bound, the refresh's retry and timeout values, the token monitor's interval (AU-07's ISSUE part; its first check is A04-02), the monitor's thresholds; no absolute machine path in any script; product codes taken from the resolver. No numerical value is chosen here." Applies: FRULES 1.4, as applied to A01 C23; A04-07 (config.yaml-driven interval), A04-37. Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT, WITH QUALIFICATION" (ChatGPT's suggested choice). Qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "Accept the principle that operationally variable settings should be centrally configured and validated rather than scattered as source literals, consistent with FRULES 1.4 and Rama's explicit A04-07 input that the token-monitor interval is config.yaml-driven, not hard-coded. Do not choose numerical values, units, ranges or defaults here. Avoid making every constant configurable indiscriminately: true invariants and protocol-defined constants may remain code constants. Absolute machine-specific paths must not be relied on; product codes should come from the authoritative resolver."

- **The 20 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items:** no treatment. MATCH records have no row and no treatment.
